### Quick Practice (116 MCQs)

1. **Select the option related to third term: 5 : 26 :: 7 : ?**
   - A) 50
   - B) 55
   - C) 48
   - D) 100
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Pattern: n : n^2 + 1. 5^2 + 1 = 26. Similarly, (7)^2 + 1 = 50. Why others are wrong: Option B gives 55; Option C gives 48; Option D doubles value.

## Number Series, Letter Series & Missing Number (Double Differences, Prime Squares, Alternating Series, Matrix Grids)

### 1. Topic Introduction
Comprehensive SSC CGL/CHSL study notes and deductive strategy for **Number Series, Letter Series & Missing Number (Double Differences, Prime Squares, Alternating Series, Matrix Grids)**. Detailed breakdown of TCS exam patterns, question distribution over 2018-2024, and high-yield rules.

### 2. Core Concepts Explained Simply
Fundamental principles, intuitive explanations, and logical foundations required to solve questions in Number Series, Letter Series & Missing Number (Double Differences, Prime Squares, Alternating Series, Matrix Grids).

- Rule 3: Follow strict sequential steps in series and pattern decoding.
- Rule 4: Verify boundary conditions and corner cases.

### Quick Practice (116 MCQs)

1. **Find the next number in series: 2, 6, 12, 20, 30, ?**
   - A) 42
   - B) 40
   - C) 46
   - D) 52
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Differences are +4, +6, +8, +10. Next difference is +12. 30 + 12 = 42. Why others are wrong: Option B subtracts 2; Option C adds 4; Option D adds 10.

## Blood Relations (Family Tree Diagrams, Coded Relations A+B means A is mother, Pointing to a photograph)

### 1. Topic Introduction

### Quick Practice (116 MCQs)

1. **Pointing to a photograph, a person says, 'He is the son of the only son of my grandfather.' How is the person related to the boy?**
   - A) Father
   - B) Uncle
   - C) Brother
   - D) Grandfather
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: 'Only son of my grandfather' = Speaker's father. 'Son of my father' = Speaker himself (or brother). Speaker is the Father. Why others are wrong: Option B is Uncle; Option C is Brother; Option D is Grandfather.

## Direction and Distance (Cardinal Directions, Shadow Concepts, Angle Rotations, Shortest Path Pythagoras)

### 1. Topic Introduction
Comprehensive SSC CGL/CHSL study notes and deductive strategy for **Direction and Distance (Cardinal Directions, Shadow Concepts, Angle Rotations, Shortest Path Pythagoras)**. Detailed breakdown of TCS exam patterns, question distribution over 2018-2024, and high-yield rules.

### 2. Core Concepts Explained Simply
Fundamental principles, intuitive explanations, and logical foundations required to solve questions in Direction and Distance (Cardinal Directions, Shadow Concepts, Angle Rotations, Shortest Path Pythagoras).

### 3. Important Facts, Formulas, Rules & Identities

- Rule 2: Use elimination techniques for option selection.
- Rule 3: Follow strict sequential steps in series and pattern decoding.
- Rule 4: Verify boundary conditions and corner cases.

### Quick Practice (116 MCQs)

1. **A man walks 3 km North, then turns Right and walks 4 km. Find the shortest distance from the starting point.**
   - A) 5 km

## Syllogism (Venn Diagram Method, 'Only a few', 'Some not', Possibility Cases, Either-Or conditions)

### 1. Topic Introduction
Comprehensive SSC CGL/CHSL study notes and deductive strategy for **Syllogism (Venn Diagram Method, 'Only a few', 'Some not', Possibility Cases, Either-Or conditions)**. Detailed breakdown of TCS exam patterns, question distribution over 2018-2024, and high-yield rules.

### 2. Core Concepts Explained Simply
Fundamental principles, intuitive explanations, and logical foundations required to solve questions in Syllogism (Venn Diagram Method, 'Only a few', 'Some not', Possibility Cases, Either-Or conditions).

### 3. Important Facts, Formulas, Rules & Identities
- Rule 1: Always establish clear positional relationships and reference axes.
- Rule 2: Use elimination techniques for option selection.
- Rule 3: Follow strict sequential steps in series and pattern decoding.
- Rule 4: Verify boundary conditions and corner cases.

### 4. Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams
Common trap options, examiner tricks, alternative logic patterns, and quick deduction shortcuts.

### 5. Differences Between Similar Terms / Confusing Rules
| Concept A | Concept B | Core Difference |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Pattern 1 | Pattern 2 | Primary application context |
| Condition 1 | Condition 2 | Variable orientation shifts |

### 6. Examples & Step-by-Step Worked Problems / Applications
Detailed step-by-step worked examples demonstrating standard methods vs 10-second exam shortcut tricks.

### 7. Previous-Year Exam Perspective
Analysis of 2018–2024 TCS trends, question frequency breakdown, and priority weightage in Tier-1 & Tier-2 exams.

### 8. Quick Revision Points, Shortcuts & Mnemonics
High-density bullet points for quick memory triggers and last-day revision pointers.

### Quick Practice (115 MCQs)

1. **Statements: All cats are dogs. All dogs are birds. Conclusions: I. All cats are birds. II. Some birds are cats.**
   - A) Both I and II follow
   - B) Only I follows
   - C) Only II follows
   - D) Neither I nor II follows
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Cats subset of Dogs subset of Birds. Both conclusion I and II are valid. Why others are wrong: Option B omits II; Option C omits I; Option D rejects both.

## Order, Ranking & Sitting Arrangement (Linear, Dual Row, Circular Inward/Outward, Square Table)

### 1. Topic Introduction
Comprehensive SSC CGL/CHSL study notes and deductive strategy for **Order, Ranking & Sitting Arrangement (Linear, Dual Row, Circular Inward/Outward, Square Table)**. Detailed breakdown of TCS exam patterns, question distribution over 2018-2024, and high-yield rules.

### 2. Core Concepts Explained Simply
Fundamental principles, intuitive explanations, and logical foundations required to solve questions in Order, Ranking & Sitting Arrangement (Linear, Dual Row, Circular Inward/Outward, Square Table).

### 3. Important Facts, Formulas, Rules & Identities
- Rule 1: Always establish clear positional relationships and reference axes.
- Rule 2: Use elimination techniques for option selection.
- Rule 3: Follow strict sequential steps in series and pattern decoding.
- Rule 4: Verify boundary conditions and corner cases.

### 4. Related Concepts Frequently Asked in SSC Exams
Common trap options, examiner tricks, alternative logic patterns, and quick deduction shortcuts.

### 6. Examples & Step-by-Step Worked Problems / Applications

### 7. Previous-Year Exam Perspective

### 8. Quick Revision Points, Shortcuts & Mnemonics
High-density bullet points for quick memory triggers and last-day revision pointers.

### Quick Practice (115 MCQs)

1. **In a row of 40 students, Rohan is 15th from the left end. What is his position from the right end?**
   - A) 26th
   - B) 25th
   - C) 27th
   - D) 24th
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Position from right = Total - Left + 1 = 40 - 15 + 1 = 26th. Why others are wrong: Option B omits +1 (25th); Option C adds 2; Option D subtracts 2.

### Quick Practice (115 MCQs)

1. **In a class of 50 students, 30 play Cricket, 25 play Football, and 10 play both. How many play neither?**
   - A) 5
   - B) 10
   - C) 7
   - D) 3
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Total playing at least one = 30 + 25 - 10 = 45. Neither = 50 - 45 = 5. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 5; Option C adds 2; Option D subtracts 2.

   - B) 15
   - C) 12
   - D) 8
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Total playing at least one = 38 + 33 - 13 = 58. Neither = 68 - 58 = 10. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 5; Option C adds 2; Option D subtracts 2.

110. **In a class of 69 students, 39 play Cricket, 34 play Football, and 14 play both. How many play neither?**
   - A) 10
   - B) 15
   - C) 12
   - D) 8
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Total playing at least one = 39 + 34 - 14 = 59. Neither = 69 - 59 = 10. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 5; Option C adds 2; Option D subtracts 2.

### Quick Practice (115 MCQs)

1. **Find the angle between the hour hand and minute hand of a clock at 4:20.**
   - A) 10.0 degrees
   - B) 20.0 degrees
   - C) 5.0 degrees
   - D) 20.0 degrees
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Angle = |30H - 5.5M| = |30(4) - 5.5(20)| = 10.0 degrees. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 10; Option C subtracts 5; Option D doubles angle.

   - A) 40.0 degrees

   - Explanation: Angle = |30H - 5.5M| = |30(4) - 5.5(20)| = 10.0 degrees. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 10; Option C subtracts 5; Option D doubles angle.

## Statement & Conclusions / Critical Reasoning (Assumptions, Course of Action, Cause and Effect, Strong/Weak Arguments)

### 1. Topic Introduction
Comprehensive SSC CGL/CHSL study notes and deductive strategy for **Statement & Conclusions / Critical Reasoning (Assumptions, Course of Action, Cause and Effect, Strong/Weak Arguments)**. Detailed breakdown of TCS exam patterns, question distribution over 2018-2024, and high-yield rules.

### 2. Core Concepts Explained Simply
Fundamental principles, intuitive explanations, and logical foundations required to solve questions in Statement & Conclusions / Critical Reasoning (Assumptions, Course of Action, Cause and Effect, Strong/Weak Arguments).

### Quick Practice (115 MCQs)

1. **Statement: 'Post this notice on the bulletin board.' - Director.
Assumptions: I. People read notices on the board. II. The company has a notice board.**
   - A) Both I and II are implicit
   - B) Only I is implicit
   - C) Only II is implicit
   - D) Neither I nor II is implicit
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Requesting to post a notice assumes people read it (I) and that a notice board exists (II). Both are implicit. Why others are wrong: Option B omits II; Option C omits I; Option D rejects both.

1. **Choose the correct mirror image of the word 'SSC' when mirror is placed on the right side:**
   - A) ƆƧƧ
   - B) SSC
   - C) ƆSS
   - D) ƧƧƆ
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: Vertical mirror reverses Left-Right order and letter orientations: S->Ƨ, S->Ƨ, C->Ɔ. Result: ƆƧƧ. Why others are wrong: Option B is unreflected original; Option C fails to reflect S; Option D keeps original letter order.

### Quick Practice (115 MCQs)

1. **A cube of side 4 cm is painted red on all faces and cut into 1 cm cubes. How many small cubes have 2 faces painted?**
   - A) 24
   - B) 28
   - C) 20
   - D) 8
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: 2-face painted cubes = 12(n - 2) = 12(4 - 2) = 24. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 4; Option C subtracts 4; Option D gives 3-face painted count (8).

2. **A cube of side 5 cm is painted red on all faces and cut into 1 cm cubes. How many small cubes have 2 faces painted?**
   - A) 36
   - B) 40
   - C) 32
   - D) 8
   - **Answer: A**
   - Explanation: 2-face painted cubes = 12(n - 2) = 12(5 - 2) = 36. Why others are wrong: Option B adds 4; Option C subtracts 4; Option D gives 3-face painted count (8).
